schema\n\n### System plane (`_tv_*`) — via `world.query()`\n\n| Table | Purpose |\n|-------|---------|\n| `_tv_view` | TaskView identity (`view_id`, `revision`) |\n| `_tv_relations` | Relation `bom_item_id` |\n| `part_type` | `part`, `part_type` |\n| `rated_voltage` | `part` → `part_id`, `volts` |\n| `requires_voltage` | `bom_item` → `bom_item_id`, `volts` |\n| `requires_type` | `bom_item` → `bom_item_id`, `part_type` |\n| `requires_temperature` | `bom_item`, `minimum_c`, `maximum_c` |\n| `temperature_range` | `part`, `minimum_c`, `maximum_c` |\n| `lifecycle` | `part`, `state` |\n| `listing_of` | `listing` → `listing_id`, `part` → `part_id` |\n| `listing_availability` | `listing`, `state` |\n| `manufacturer_part`, `supplier_listing`, `offered_by`, `deployment_environment`, `candidate_replacement`, `acceptable_replacement` | (supporting) |\n\n**DERIVED (materialized SQL):**\n\n| Relation | Definition (from `_tv_derivations`) | Inputs |\n|----------|-------------------------------------|--------|\n| **`spec_conflict`** | `SELECT part_id, 'rated_voltage_v' FROM rated_voltage GROUP BY part_id HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT volts) > 1` | `rated_voltage` |\n| `voltage_compatible` | part type match + `rated_voltage.volts = requires_voltage.volts` | `part_type`, `requires_type`, `requires_voltage`, `rated_voltage` |\n| temperature_compatible`, excluding `lifecycle.state = 'discontinued'` | `voltage_compatible`, `temperature_compatible`, `lifecycle` |\n\n**Referent IDs** are already prefixed in column values: `part:X100`, `bom:BOM-A`, `listing:AC-X100`, `context:indoor_panel`. Human labels live in `_tv_referents`.\n\n**Current conflict in this World:** one row in `spec_conflict`: `(part:X100, rated_voltage_v)`.\n\n---\n\n## Query strategy for specification-conflict review\n\nAligned with `prompt.txt` and validated by trial_004 logic.\n\n### 1. Seed conflicts\n```sql\nSELECT part_id, property FROM spec_conflict\n```\nCheck `world.stale_relations()` does not include `spec_conflict` or `eligible_part` (both COMPLETE in this DB).\n\n### 2. Per conflict row (`part`, `property`)\n\n**`values`** — distinct conflicting observations:\n```sql\nSELECT volts FROM rated_voltage WHERE part_id = ?\n```\n\n**`matching_bom_items`** — BOM items whose voltage requirement is satisfied by at least one of the part’s observations and whose required part type matches:\n```sql\nSELECT DISTINCT requirement.bom_item_id\nFROM requires_voltage AS requirement\nJOIN requires_type AS required_kind\n  ON required_kind.bom_item_id = requirement.bom_item_id\nJOIN part_type AS actual_kind\n  ON actual_kind.part_type = required_kind.part_type\nJOIN rated_voltage AS observation\n  ON observation.part_id = actual_kind.part_id\n AND observation.volts = requirement.volts\nWHERE actual_kind.part_id = ?\n```\nFor `part:X100` → `bom:BOM-A`, `bom:BOM-B` (both require 24 V; 28 V does not match any BOM requirement).\n\n**`eligible_bom_items`** — fully eligible (voltage + temperature + not discontinued):\n```sql\nSELECT bom_item_id FROM eligible_part WHERE part_id = ?\n```\n\n**`listings_of_part`**:\n```sql\nSELECT owned.listing_id, status.state\nFROM listing_of AS owned\nJOIN listing_availability AS status\n  ON status.listing_id = owned.listing_id\nWHERE owned.part_id = ?\n```\n\n**`observation_sources`** — for each distinct `volts`, call:\n```python\nworld.inspect_tuple(\"rated_voltage\", {\"part\": part, \"volts\": volts})\n```\nExtract `grounding` entries with `kind == \"SOURCE\"`. Parse `detail` JSON field `\"source\"` (fallback: strip `fixture://` prefix from `reference`). For X100:\n- 24 → `manufacturer.csv`\n- 28 → `suppliers.json`\n\n**`review`**: constant `\"conflicting_rated_voltage_observations\"` when `property == \"rated_voltage_v\"`.\n\n### 3. Sort output\n- Conflicts: `(part, property)`\n- Lists: lexicographic\n- `values`: numeric ascending\n- `observation_sources`: by `volts`, then `source`\n\n---\n\n## Derivation chain (eligibility context)\n\n```\nrequires_type + requires_voltage + rated_voltage + part_type\n  → voltage_compatible\nrequires_type + requires_temperature + temperature_range + part_type\n  → temperature_compatible\nvoltage_compatible ∩ temperature_compatible ∩ lifecycle≠discontinued\n  → eligible_part\n\nrated_voltage (multiple volts per part)\n  → spec_conflict\n```\n\n---\n\n## Practical notes\n\n1. Use `world.relation_schema(name)[\"roles\"]` to map role names → SQL column names (e.g. `part` → `part_id`).\n2. `world.query_semantic()` cannot expose `_assertion_id`; use `world.inspect_tuple()` for per-observation provenance.\n3. `world.obligations()` reports missing judgments (here: `acceptable_replacement` for `part:X110` replacing `part:X160` in `context:indoor_panel`) — separate from spec conflicts.\n4.
